the minute-taker shall sign on the minutes for confirmation. If a Director has different opinions on the minutes, he/she
may attach an explanation to his/her confirmation. The minutes of the Board meeting shall be properly preserved as
important files of the Company, with a retention period of over 10 years.

The Board and each Director also have separate and independent access to the senior management whenever
necessary.

RISK MANAGEMENT AND INTERNAL CONTROLS
The Board acknowledges its responsibility for the risk management and internal control systems and reviewing their
effectiveness. Such systems are designed to manage rather than eliminate the risk of failure to achieve business
objectives, and can only provide reasonable but not absolute assurance against material misstatement or loss.

The Board has the overall responsibility for evaluating and determining the nature and extent of the risks it is willing to
take in achieving the Company’s strategic objectives, and establishing and maintaining appropriate and effective risk
management and internal control systems.

The Audit Committee assists the Board in leading the management and overseeing the design, implementation and
monitoring of the risk management and internal control systems.

We have established a three lines of defense model for risk management and internal control mechanisms.

The first line of defense consists of the Company’s various business and functional departments, which are responsible
for daily business operations and management, as well as the design and implementation of relevant controls to
address risks. Each department identifies risk events based on internal and external environments, and continuously
improves and strengthens the Company’s internal control rules and regulations system accordingly. Department heads
ensure that the requirements of each daily control activities are effectively implemented through internal training,
dissemination, and self-inspection within their departments.

The second line of defense is implemented by the internal control team, which is responsible for formulating policies
related to the Company’s risk management and internal control, uniformly planning and carrying out the development of
the risk management and internal control systems, and assisting the first line of defense in establishing and enhancing
its risk management and internal control systems. In this capacity, it performs supervisory duties to provide reasonable
assurance that the risk management and internal control efforts of the first line of defense are effectively implemented.

The third line of defense is implemented by the internal audit team, which is responsible for providing independent
evaluations of the effectiveness of the Company’s risk management and internal control systems, and supervising
management in the continuous improvement and enhancement of risk management and internal control practices and
shall directly report work progress and results to the Audit Committee.




54     LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD.   ANNUAL REPORT 2025
Corporate Governance Report




The general manager’s office supervises the risk prevention and control efforts of all departments, coordinates cross-
departmental risk prevention and control, incorporates risk responsibilities and indicators into the key performance
indicators (KPIs) of the primary responsible persons of each department, ensuring the sustained and healthy operation
of the risk prevention and control mechanism.

We endeavour to uphold the integrity of our business by maintaining risk management and internal control systems
within our organizational structure. Our internal control system and procedures are designed to meet our specific
business needs and to minimise our risk exposure.

We engaged an independent internal control consultant (the “Internal Control Consultant”) to perform a review
of the adequacy and effectiveness of the risk management and internal control systems over our major business
processes. The contents relate to, among other matters, the control environment, risk management, information and
communication, operational-level controls (such as revenue, procurement, inventory, fixed assets, fund management,
expense reimbursement, financial reporting, human resources, etc.) and internal supervision to enhance the internal
control system of our Group. The Internal Control Consultant has not identified any material findings which may have
material impact on the effectiveness of our internal control system.

Based on the result of the Internal Control Review, the Board, as supported by the Audit Committee, reviewed the risk
management and internal control systems, including the financial, operational and compliance controls, for the year
ended December 31, 2025, and considered that such systems are effective and adequate. The annual review also
covered the financial reporting, internal audit function, adequacy of resources, staff qualifications and experiences,
training programmes and budget of the Company’s accounting, internal audit and financial reporting functions.

WHISTLEBLOWING POLICY
The Company has adopted arrangement to facilitate employees and other stakeholders to raise concerns, in
confidence, about possible improprieties in financial reporting, internal control or other matters.

The Audit Committee of the Company shall review such arrangement regularly and ensure that proper arrangements
are in place for fair and independent investigation of these matters and for appropriate follow-up action.

INSIDE INFORMATION
The Company has developed its disclosure policy which provides a general guide to the Company’s Directors, senior
management and relevant employees in handling confidential information, monitoring information disclosure and
responding to enquiries. Control procedures have been implemented to ensure that unauthorized access and use of
inside information are strictly prohibited.

POTENTIAL CONFLICT OF INTERESTS BETWEEN THE GROUP AND THE CONTROLLING
SHAREHOLDERS
As part of our corporate governance policies, the independent non-executive Directors review, on an annual basis,
whether there are any conflict of interests between the Group and the Controlling Shareholders and provide advice to
protect the interests of our minority Shareholders. During the year ended December 31, 2025, the independent non-
executive Directors have reviewed the necessary information. Given that (i) none of the Controlling Shareholders or
their associates had any interests in any business which competes or is likely to compete, directly or indirectly, with the
businesses of the Group; (ii) the Group did not enter into any connected transaction, which are subject to the reporting,
annual review and announcement requirements under the Chapter 14A of the Listing Rules, with the Controlling
Shareholders or their associates; and (iii) the Audit Committee had reviewed the Group’s internal control systems for
the year ended December 31, 2025 and considered that such systems are effective and adequate, the independent
non-executive Directors were of the view that there were no potential conflict of interests between the Group and the
Controlling Shareholders during the year ended December 31, 2025.

and Supervisors. Specific enquiries have been made to all the Directors and Supervisors, and the Directors and

SHAREHOLDERS’ RIGHTS
To safeguard Shareholders’ interests and rights, separate resolution should be proposed for each substantially separate
issue at shareholders’ meetings, including the election of Directors. All resolutions put forward at shareholders’
meetings will be voted on by poll pursuant to the Listing Rules and poll results will be posted on the websites of the
Company and of the Stock Exchange after each shareholders’ meeting.

Procedures for Shareholders to Convene Extraordinary General Meeting
According to the Articles of Association, Shareholders who individually or jointly hold more than 10% of the Company’s
voting Shares at the proposed extraordinary general meeting or Shareholders’ class meeting have the right to require
the Board to convene an extraordinary general meeting in accordance with the following procedures:

(1)    to sign one or more written requests of the same format and content to propose the Board to convene an
       extraordinary general meeting and state the subject matter of the meeting. The Board shall give a written reply as
       to whether or not it agrees to convene an extraordinary general meeting within ten (10) days after receiving the
       aforesaid written request.

(2)    if the Board agrees to convene an extraordinary general meeting, the notice of the meeting shall be issued
       within five (5) days after the adoption of the relevant board resolution. Any changes to the original requisition
       set out in the notice are subject to prior consent of the Shareholders concerned. The Shareholdings referred to
       above shall be maintained until the announcement of the shareholders’ meeting resolution.



58     LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD.   ANNUAL REPORT 2025
Corporate Governance Report




(3)    if the Board does not agree to convene an extraordinary general meeting or fails to furnish any reply within
       ten (10) days after receiving such requisition, Shareholders individually or jointly holding 10% or more of the
       shares carrying the right to vote at the meeting sought to be held shall be entitled to propose to the Supervisory
       Committee to convene an extraordinary general meeting, provided that such proposal shall be made in writing.
       In the event that the Supervisory Committee agrees to convene an extraordinary general meeting, the notice of
       the meeting shall be issued within five (5) days after receipt of the request. Any changes to the original proposal
       set out in the notice are subject to prior consent of the Shareholders concerned. Failure of the Supervisory
       Committee to issue a notice of meeting within the prescribed time limit shall be deemed as failure of the
       Supervisory Committee to convene and preside over a shareholders’ meeting, in which case Shareholders
       individually or jointly holding 10% or more of the Company’s shares for ninety (90) consecutive days or more
       may convene and preside over the meeting.

Any reasonable expenses incurred by Shareholders or the Supervisory Committee in convening and presiding over
a meeting by reason of the failure of the Board to duly convene a meeting as requested above shall be borne by the
Company and shall be set off against sums owed by the Company to the Directors in default.

Procedures for Shareholders to Nominate Candidates of Directors
Written notices specifying the intention to nominate a person for election as a Director and acceptance of such
nomination by such person, as well as the written information on such person, shall be sent to the Company no earlier
than the day after dispatch of the notice of the shareholders’ meeting and no later than seven (7) days prior to the date
of such meeting. The minimum length of period during the nomination and acceptance of such nomination shall not be
less than seven (7) days.

Based on this, if a Shareholder of the Company intends to propose any person for election as a Director, the following
documents shall be effectively delivered to the Company’s principal place of business in Hong Kong currently at 19th
Floor, Golden Centre, 188 Des Voeux Road Central, Hong Kong, including: (i) the signed notice of the intention to
propose the candidate for election as a Director in shareholders’ meeting; and (ii) the signed notice of the candidate
indicating his or her willingness to accept the election, together with (a) information about the candidate required to be
disclosed under Rule 13.51(2) of the Listing Rules, and (b) the written consent indicating the consent of the candidate to
release his or her personal information.

•        With an aim of providing stable and reasonable returns to the Shareholders.

•        The Company shall distribute dividends twice per annum. The amount of each dividend distribution by

         statements as of the end of the current period, and the distribution shall be made in cash.

•        When proposing the declaration of dividends, the Board shall mainly consider the following factors: the

         existence of significant capital expenditure plans, as well as any other conditions or factors deemed relevant by
         the Board.

•        The Policy and the declaration and/or payment of dividends shall be in the best interests of the Company and its
         shareholders, and shall comply with all applicable laws and regulations.

ABOUT THIS REPORT
This report aims to provide an objective and transparent disclosure of the environmental, social and governance
(hereinafter referred to as “ESG”) practices and performance of Laopu Gold Co., Ltd. (hereinafter referred to as “Laopu
Gold” or the “Company” or “we”) and its subsidiaries (the “Group”) in 2025. For detailed information on corporate
governance, it is recommended to read in conjunction with the Corporate Governance Report section in this annual
report.

This Report has been prepared in accordance with the Environmental, Social and Governance Reporting Guide as set
out in Appendix C2 to the Main Board Listing Rules of The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited (hereinafter referred to as
“Hong Kong Stock Exchange”), and fully considers its reporting principles, namely materiality, quantitative, balance and
consistency:

•      Materiality: This report gives a report on the ESG issues that the board of directors has determined to have a
       significant impact on all stakeholders.

•      Quantitative: This report discloses relevant quantitative data in the ESG area, supplemented with explanatory
       text.

•      Balance: This report provides an objective presentation of the Company’s ESG performance during this Reporting
       Period.

•      Consistency: Unless otherwise stated, a consistent disclosure and statistical methodology is applied for each
       Reporting Period.

This Report covers the management policies and performance of the Group in terms of ESG from January 1, 2025 to
December 31, 2025 (i.e., the “Reporting Period”). To enhance the completeness and comparability of the report, some
information appropriately traces back to previous years. The group policies and data as disclosed in this Report, unless
otherwise specified, encompass the Company and its subsidiaries.

BOARD STATEMENT
The Board of Directors, as the highest governing body responsible for the Group’s ESG matters, exercises
comprehensive oversight over the Company’s ESG affairs by regularly reviewing ESG plans, hearing special work
reports, and supervising implementation, thereby ensuring that ESG concepts are deeply integrated into all aspects of
operation and management.

The Board of Directors, taking into account industry characteristics and the Company’s core business, identifies
material ESG issues through internal assessments and communication with stakeholders. It evaluates their potential
impact on business operations, risk control, and long-term value creation, prioritizes them, formulates corresponding
management strategies, and integrates related risks into the overall corporate risk management system for coordinated
control.

Furthermore, the Board regularly reviews the Company’s progress towards its ESG objectives, analyzes deviations from
targets and areas for improvement, ensuring that ESG efforts are closely linked to the Company’s long-term strategy
and business development, and jointly promoting the synergistic growth of commercial value and sustainable value.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report




ESG MANAGEMENT
ESG Governance Structure
The Group regards ESG as one of the core drivers of its long-term development. In the future, the Group will continue to
deepen the implementation of ESG concepts, build long-term trust with all parties, and build the world’s top gold jewelry
brand with unparalleled competitiveness.

The Group has established a clear ESG governance structure, committed to fulfilling corporate social responsibilities and
achieving sustainable development across all levels of its business operations.

The Board of Directors, as the highest governing and decision-making body for the Group’s ESG matters, exercises
comprehensive management over the Company’s ESG affairs. Concurrently, all directors receive annual special
training related to ESG, covering topics such as ESG trends, industry developments, and regulatory requirements, to
continuously enhance their competency in ESG management.

The Strategy Committee of the Board is responsible for overall planning and guidance of ESG-related work,
including: researching and resolving the Company’s ESG-related strategies, objectives, policies and systems, risks
and opportunities (including climate-related risks and opportunities) and significant matters, reviewing ESG-related
reports, supervising and inspecting the implementation of ESG work and the progress of objectives, and making
recommendations. Regarding the aforementioned matters, the Strategy Committee of the Board reports to the Board
and provides relevant opinions in a timely manner (at least once a year).

The Audit Committee of the Board is responsible for assisting the Board in overseeing the identification, assessment,
response and supervision of ESG-related risks and opportunities (including climate-related risks), and reviewing the
effectiveness of ESG-related risk management and internal controls. Regarding the aforementioned matters, the Audit
Committee of the Board reports to the Board and provides relevant opinions in a timely manner (at least once a year).

To drive the implementation of the Group’s ESG work, the Group has established an ESG working group. This group
is responsible for coordinating the implementation of specific tasks according to ESG objectives, executing ESG
management policies, monitoring progress towards objectives, ensuring the adequacy and effectiveness of ESG-
related measures; conducting stakeholder communication and responding to concerns; coordinating the collection,
consolidation, analysis and monitoring of ESG performance data, and preparing ESG reports. Regarding the
aforementioned matters, the ESG working group reports to the Strategy Committee and the Audit Committee in a timely
manner (at least once a year).

PRODUCTS AND SERVICES
Cultural Inheritance
Promote the creative transformation and innovative development of China’s outstanding traditional
culture
Established in 2009, Laopu Gold is a brand certified by the China Gold Association as “the first brand in China to
promote the concept of ‘heritage gold jewelry’ and the leading professional brand of heritage handmade gold artifacts
in China”.

Laopu Gold consistently takes classical culture as its product theme, deeply extracting the essence of China’s
outstanding traditional culture and oriental aesthetics, refining classic symbols and imagery imbued with both historical
depth and emotional resonance. Laopu Gold takes elements with auspicious meanings and philosophical depth, such as
gourds and the crossed vajra, and gives them contemporary expression through innovative design language, allowing
them to radiate renewed cultural vitality and artistic charm in the new consumption environment, continuously gaining
consumer favor and resonance.

The heritage gold crafting techniques passed down by Laopu Gold are the legacy of imperial goldsmithing from various
Chinese dynasties, primarily encompassing three major crafts, i.e., “ancient casting method, handcrafted fine gold
detailing, and handcrafted gold finishing”. Each category includes multiple heritage gold crafting techniques, among
which the “filigree and inlay” technique was inscribed on the National Intangible Cultural Heritage List in June 2008 and
continues to be utilized, inherited, and developed in Laopu Gold’s contemporary innovations.

Laopu Gold has achieved systematic high-quality inheritance of Chinese culture and intangible cultural heritage crafts.
Through the international expansion of its brand and market, it enables the world to appreciate the beauty of China’s
culture and intangible cultural heritage crafts.

Pioneer a new era for the industry
Since establishment of its heritage gold jewelry business in 2009, Laopu Gold has not only served as a drafting unit for
the two group standards of “Fine gold adornment with Chinese traditional gold and silver craft” (《古法金飾品》團體標準 )
and “Fine gold adornment setting with diamonds and made of Chinese traditional gold and silver craft”(《古法金鑲嵌鑽
石飾品》團體標準 ) published by the China Gold Association, but has also driven the development of heritage gold with
multiple industry firsts. Laopu Gold is the first brand to introduce “diamond-inlaid pure gold jewelry”, and the pioneer
in launching innovative craft products such as “pure gold enamel products” and “gold-body lacquerware”, significantly
leading and spurring the rapid surge in market demand for heritage gold products. According to Frost & Sullivan, the
market size of heritage gold jewelry in Chinese mainland was about RMB13 billion in 2018, and is expected to reach
approximately RMB421.4 billion by 2028, with an expected compound annual growth rate of 21.8%.

By integrating China’s intangible cultural heritage gold craftsmanship with international fashion aesthetics and adhering
to original design, Laopu Gold continuously creates high-end heritage gold products with distinct cultural value. Laopu
Gold has driven the industry’s shift from a low-quality involution to high-quality economic development, ushering in a
new era for the industry.




                                                                          ANNUAL REPORT 2025   LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD    65
Environmental, Social and Governance Report




Quality and Product Integrity
The Group regards quality and product integrity control as one of the cores of the product strength of Laopu Gold. We
strictly comply with domestic and international quality management requirements, including the Product Quality Law
of the People’s Republic of China, and have established internal quality control systems such as the Product Quality
Management Process, the Product and Raw Material Inspection Management Policy, and the Product Quality Standards,
strictly adhering to quality standards. Every product from Laopu Gold undergoes rigorous quality control management
throughout the entire process, from “design, sampling and processing” to “warehousing and shelve placement”
and inspection from the National Jewelry Quality Testing and Inspection Center (國家首飾質量檢驗檢測中心 ), the
certificate of which is ensured to be attached with every item stored and displayed in Chinese mainland. From internal
management to external product certification, we provide consumers with a full range of product quality assurance. A
multi-tiered and stringent quality control system ensures that we maintain our competitive edge in product excellence.

During the Reporting Period, the Group did not engage in any significant violations of laws and regulations related
to product quality liability. Quantitative data relating to our products and services is presented in the “ESG Key
Performance”.

Customer Services
High-quality customer service is paramount to the brand positioning of Laopu Gold. We strictly comply with domestic
and international laws and regulations such as the Law of the People’s Republic of China on the Protection of Consumer
Rights and Interests (《中華人民共和國消費者權益保護法》) and the Measures for the Punishment of Acts Infringing
upon Consumers’ Rights and Interests (《侵害消費者權益行為處罰辦法》), and have formulated internal policies and
systems such as the Customer Service Manual to strictly regulate service processes and standards.

Our sales personnel provide one-on-one and personalized services, catering to every stage of the shopping experience,
from the moment customers enter the store to the final product purchase. We regularly assess the performance of our
sales personnel to maintain high-quality and consistent customer services. We have established dedicated customer
service representatives at our offline stores and e-commerce platforms, who are also responsible for responding to
and handling customer service and product feedback or complaints. We conduct monthly reviews of the feedback
submitted by our customers.

In addition, we have established a membership center and set up characteristic themed scenes such as Chinese
classical study rooms and tea rooms in more stores. This setup more thoroughly showcases our classic craftsmanship,
signature products, and brand thematic culture, offering consumers an immersive shopping experience. We also
offer a range of after-sales services to our members, including lifetime complimentary basic care services, and cord
replacement services.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report




Information Security and Privacy Protection
The Group considers data security and privacy protection to be fundamental to the stable operation of the enterprise.
We strictly comply with relevant laws and regulations in the locations where we operate such as the Cyber Security
Law of the PRC, the Data Security Law of the PRC, the Law of the PRC on the Protection of Personal Information, the
Personal Data (Privacy) Ordinance in Hong Kong, the Law on Network Security (No. 13/2019) in Macau, and the Law on
the Protection of Personal Data (No. 8/2005) in Macau. Internally, we have formulated management regulations such as
the Personal Information Protection Management Regulations, and the Information System Security Management Basic
Outline to ensure data protection requirements cover all business processes. We continuously improve the information
security management system, take precautions against data leakage, protect the security of information and data to
the greatest extent, and ensure compliance with the above-mentioned provisions when collecting, storing, using and/
or processing customers’ personal data. We pay close attention to the policies related to user information protection
issued by ministries and commissions such as the Ministry of Industry and Information Technology of the People’s
Republic of China, the Office of the Central Cyberspace Affairs Commission, and the China Consumers Association, and
continuously update the Company’s internal information security management measures to ensure that the Company’s
information security management system complies with the latest legal and regulatory requirements.

The Group formulates user service agreements, clearly defining user information and privacy policies. Before collecting
customer information, we explicitly inform them of the purpose, scope, and methods of use. We collect and retain
the minimum necessary personal data of its customers in the database, contingent upon their voluntary consent in
accordance with laws. Without explicit authorization from the individual, it is prohibited to provide or display personal
information to third parties in any way. Customers have control over their personal data and are allowed to query,
correct, delete, or withdraw authorization.

The Group has established an Information Technology Department responsible for formulating data protection
strategies, supervising policy implementation, and coordinating cross-departmental data security matters. We have
implemented a series of measures to protect information security and customer privacy, registering data processing
activities, and adopting several protective measures at the network and data layers to ensure the security of data
stored and transmitted in our systems, such as strict internal and external network isolation policies, encrypted network
access restrictions, deployment of Web Application Firewall (WAF) programs, antivirus programs, and authorization
controls applied at both the application and server levels following the principle of least privilege. We also employ a
variety of encryption technologies to ensure data security, including full encryption of data backups and Transport Layer
Security (TLS) encryption, thus effectively preventing data theft or tampering, and ensuring the data authenticity and
integrity. We conduct an annual cybersecurity penetration test and also carry out periodic special audits of information
systems to promptly identify and rectify potential risks. The Group has obtained Level 2 certification for information
security protection, demonstrating our capability to safeguard against various external malicious attacks and swiftly
recover in the event of an attack.

Meanwhile, we have developed data breach emergency response plans, specifying incident classification, reporting
paths, and handling procedures to ensure a rapid response and mitigate impact in the event of a data breach. We
have developed disaster recovery plans for all application servers, and our cloud service provider has implemented
redundancy mechanisms and various other data loss protection and disaster recovery measures to ensure the security
and stability of our cloud-stored data.

Furthermore, we conduct ongoing privacy and data security training for all employees, including case studies and
operational specification training, to enhance the information security and privacy protection awareness of all staff.




                                                                          ANNUAL REPORT 2025   LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD    67
Environmental, Social and Governance Report




Intellectual Property Protection
Laopu Gold adheres to originality, while emphasizing product research, development, innovation and upgrade. The
continuous optimization, introduction and iteration of our products are the driving forces behind our sustained growth.
Based on our philosophy, we hold a profound respect for our own intellectual property rights as well as those of other
parties, actively manage and expand our intellectual property portfolio. Quantitative data relating to intellectual property
rights is presented in the “ESG Key Performance”.

Responsible Marketing
We strictly adhere to applicable laws, regulations and national standards in the locations where we operate relating
to responsible marketing, including the Advertising Law of the People’s Republic of China, the E-Commerce Law of
the People’s Republic of China, and the National Standard of the People’s Republic of China – Provisions and Naming
Methods for the Purity of Precious Metals in Jewelry, among others. We have established rigorous content review
processes and mechanisms, while conducting multiple checks on product labeling and all external promotional
materials to ensure that all content undergoes strict scrutiny. Our legal department also regularly conducts various
forms of communication and/or training to the Brand Management Department, the E-Commerce Department, the
Business Department, and the Supply Chain Management Department, covering case analysis and regulatory training,
so as to further strengthen responsible marketing practices. During the Reporting Period, the Group did not encounter
any material disputes or complaints relating to market promotion or label usage, nor were there any litigation cases
arising from these matters.

ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT
Laopu Gold strictly complies with relevant applicable laws and regulations such as the Environmental Protection Law
of the People’s Republic of China and the Law of the People’s Republic of China on Energy Conservation, as well as
other environmental protection regulations in the locations where it operates, fully committed to promoting a green
operational model. We have formulated and implemented rules and regulations such as the Environment Protection
Policy and the Contingency Plan for Environmental Emergencies, establishing a comprehensive environmental
management system and standardizing environmental management procedures, dedicated to putting the green
operation concept into practice.

The Group’s operational premises are primarily located in commercial office buildings (daily offices), renowned
commercial centers (boutique operations), and buildings in the economic development zones (a self-owned factory).
We are committed to embedding the concept of environmental protection deeply into all business segments and the
entire chain of products and services, actively implementing pollution prevention and control strategies, and effectively
improving environmental management efficiency. Through a top-down management approach, the Group continuously
strengthens the management of emissions and resource consumption, systematically conducts environmental risk
assessments, promptly identifies and effectively controls potential environmental risk factors, and comprehensively
enhances environmental management effectiveness.

To deepen the practice of green operations, the Group regularly conducts environmental protection training and
awareness campaigns, actively promotes the full participation of all employees in environmental management,
encourages employees to implement environmental protection measures in daily operations, integrates the concept
of green development into all processes of corporate operations, and continuously consolidates the foundation for
sustainable development.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report




Emission Management
Laopu Gold strictly complies with relevant applicable laws and regulations in the locations where we operate such
as the Law of the People’s Republic of China on the Prevention and Control of Atmospheric Pollution, the Law of the
People’s Republic of China on the Prevention and Control of Environmental Pollution by Solid Waste, the Law of the
People’s Republic of China on the Prevention and Control of Water Pollution, and the Law of the People’s Republic of
China on the Prevention and Control of Environmental Noise Pollution. We formulate and strictly adhere to management
policies and regulations covering emissions such as wastewater, exhaust gases and solid waste, and have established
a regular monitoring mechanism to continuously reduce the negative environmental impact of our operations.
Quantitative data relating to emission management is presented in the “ESG Key Performance”.

1.     Factory Production Process
       The primary production processes of Laopu Gold encompass heritage casting methods (wax ejecting,
       investment casting, core removal, sand hole repair and polishing, etc.), traditional handcrafted goldsmithing
       techniques (carving, embedding, heat treatment of enamels, etc.), overall surface treatment, and subsequent
       product processing. These processes generate only minimal pollutants. We adhere to internal systems such as
       the Environmental Protection System and Standards to implement standardized management of exhaust gases,
       wastewater, solid waste and noise generated during production. This ensures all emissions are compliantly
       treated and discharged up to standards, reduces noise pollution, and effectively avoids damage to the
       surrounding environment.

       Compliant Wastewater Discharge
       Acidic wastewater generated during production is centrally collected and properly treated through
       environmental protection equipment. Production wastewater is discharged into a collection and sedimentation
       tank. We conduct daily stratified monitoring of wastewater to ensure that wastewater meets national discharge
       standards before being discharged and engage third-party organizations for regular testing to ensure that the
       pollutant concentrations in the treated wastewater at the final discharge outlet comply with local wastewater
       discharge requirements. Our domestic sewage is treated via the industrial park’s septic tanksNote 1.

       Proper Treatment of Exhaust Gases
       We have installed exhaust gas purification devices in the factory. Air collection hoods capture organic waste
       gases (paraffin smoke) and gold melting fumes generated during production. These gases are then centrally
       treated using jewelry exhaust gas purification devices (an alkali atomization device utilizing electric mist
       elimination technology) and led to the building rooftop, at a height of no less than 20 meters, for compliant
       discharge. We also engage third-party organizations to conduct regular testing to ensure that the concentration
       of pollutants in the treated exhaust gas at the main emission point complies with local exhaust gas emission
       requirements. We are equipped with automatic monitoring equipment to track gas concentrations within the
       factory, enabling real-time alerts in case of abnormal concentrations.

       Effective Waste Management
       Hazardous waste is mainly consists of small quantities of chemical packaging containers and engine oil
       generated in our production process. We manage hazardous waste in a standardized way through the
       Hazardous Chemicals Management Policy. Waste containers and engine oil are temporarily stored in a
       hazardous waste storage room and are collected and processed centrally by engaged third-party environmental
       protection companies.

       Non-hazardous waste, primarily consisting of discarded waste gypsum, packaging bags, and slag from
       sedimentation tanks, is centrally stored in a general solid waste room. Waste gypsum is regularly collected and
       disposed of by specialized institutions, while discarded packaging bags, slag from sedimentation tanks and
       domestic waste are placed together in the park’s garbage area for centralized collection and disposal by the
       sanitation department.

       Reasonable Noise Control
       Equipment operation is the main source of noise during production. We have implemented foundational
       vibration damping measures to effectively reduce equipment noise, planned the layout of noisy equipment
       rationally, and ensured noise sources are located away from the factory boundary by situating machinery inside
       the factory buildings. Additionally, during equipment operation hours, we take measures such as closing doors
       and windows to minimize noise interference with the external environment.
Note 1: During the Reporting Period, the Company has not yet systematically collected statistics on wastewater discharge from its
        production and operation processes. In future years, it will gradually improve the collection, monitoring, and disclosure of
        environmental data, and continuously enhance its environmental management level.


                                                                                 ANNUAL REPORT 2025   LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD       69
Environmental, Social and Governance Report




2.     Daily Operations
       Wastewater generated during the daily operations of the Group’s boutiques and offices consists solely of
       domestic sewage, which is discharged into the corresponding municipal pipeline networks via the commercial
       office buildings or shopping centers. Waste primarily comprises non-hazardous materials such as discarded
       office supplies and domestic waste. We have implemented garbage classification management, entrusting a
       qualified third-party organization with the compliant disposal of recyclable waste to promote resource recycling.
       Furthermore, we regularly conduct environmental protection training and awareness campaigns to continuously
       enhance employees’ environmental protection consciousness and participation in waste sorting.

Resource Consumption Management
Laopu Gold places high importance on managing the consumption of various resources, strictly adhering to relevant
national laws, regulations and industry standards. We have formulated and rigorously follow policies and systems
covering the conservation of resources such as water, electricity, fuel and paper. The concept of resource conservation
is deeply integrated into the entire operation process, continuously driving down energy intensity and advancing the
green transformation of our operations. Quantitative data relating to resource consumption is presented in the “ESG
Key Performance”.

Energy Management
In terms of electricity usage, the Group implements refined electricity management, systematically promoting energy-
saving renovations and operational optimization. We are progressively phasing out high-energy-consumption equipment
and promoting energy-efficient electrical facilities and smart control devices (such as timer/sound-activated devices)
to adjust electricity load based on demand. We optimize temperature control plans for air conditioning in business
premises to eliminate unnecessary energy consumption. Electricity usage is closely monitored, reasonable consumption
ranges are set, and periodic assessments are conducted to ensure electricity consumption remains within appropriate
limits. For boutique operations, we scientifically plan lighting systems and strictly enforce the policy of shutting down
energy sources during non-business hours to minimize standby energy consumption and continuously improve energy
utilization efficiency.

In terms of fuel usage, this mainly pertains to gasoline used by office vehicles and few diesel used in our factory.
We gradually replace traditional vehicles with electric vehicles to effectively reduce dependence on fossil fuels and
lower carbon emission intensity. We actively promote a culture of green commuting and strictly control total gasoline
consumption, ensuring its usage remains within a reasonable range.

Water Management
Sensor-activated water-saving devices are installed in our operating premises, and a regular inspection mechanism is
implemented to prevent leaks and drips. Through continuous water conservation publicity and behavioral guidance, we
strengthen all the employees’ awareness of water saving. Concurrently, we implement dynamic monitoring of water
usage to ensure water resources are always utilized within a reasonable range.

Paper Management
We deeply promote a paperless office, extensively utilizing collaborative office systems and electronic approval
processes. We advocate double-sided printing and the recycling of wastepaper and reduce the consumption of items
like disposable paper cups, systematically lowering paper usage intensity. Furthermore, we have implemented an
electronic invoice system across all boutiques. Through guiding green consumption, we have significantly increased the
proportion of electronic invoices used, markedly reducing the consumption of paper receipts, thereby extending the
concept of green service to the consumer end.

Packaging Materials
The Group has established a green evaluation and management system for packaging materials, systematically assessing
their environmental attributes from a product life-cycle perspective. We continuously optimize packaging design,
prioritizing the use of environmentally friendly, recyclable, and biodegradable materials, significantly reducing the
proportion of plastic used to lessen the environmental burden at the source. Concurrently, we extend green packaging
requirements to supply chain management, encouraging upstream suppliers and product transport service providers
to adopt low-carbon, environmentally friendly packaging materials, thus systematically implementing resource
conservation goals.




70     LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD.   ANNUAL REPORT 2025
Environmental, Social and Governance Report




Environment and Natural Resources
The Group is committed to minimizing the impact of its production and operations on the environmental and natural
resources, and takes proactive measures and actions to reduce such impact to the greatest extent possible.

Our production process involves manufacturing gold jewelry. Pursuant to the Categorized Management List for
Environmental Impact Assessment of Construction Projects (2021 Edition) released by the Ministry of Ecology and
Environmental Protection, our production process does not produce substantial heavy pollutants, and our products
are not named in the “High Pollution and High Environmental Risk” product list of the Comprehensive Environmental
Protection Catalog (2021 Edition) released by the Ministry of Ecology and Environmental Protection.

Our operational premises are primarily located in commercial office buildings (offices), renowned commercial centers
(boutiques), and economic development buildings (a self-owned factory). These locations do not involve any damage
to surrounding natural resources. Their operations comply with local environmental laws and regulations. When
selecting office locations, we prioritize buildings that meet green building standards. Currently, our leased office spaces
in Beijing and Shanghai have been certified as LEED Platinum Buildings, and some of our boutiques are also situated
in commercial complexes that have received green building certification. During the Reporting Period, there were no
serious environmental violations or administrative penalties that would have a significant impact on the environment.

Addressing Climate Change
Governance
The Group has organically integrated climate change-related matters into its ESG governance structure. The Board’s
Strategy Committee holds the responsibility for climate risk and opportunity management. It is responsible for
reviewing and approving the Group’s overall climate change strategy, receiving an annual report on the assessment
results of climate change risks and opportunities, corresponding strategies and progress against targets, and providing
recommendations for improvement. The Board’s Audit Committee assists in overseeing the processes for identifying,
assessing, and responding to climate-related risks and opportunities. The ESG working group, in coordination with
various business departments, is responsible for the specific implementation of climate-related work and monitors the
achievement of relevant targets. Through this interconnected governance mechanism, we ensure the adequacy and
effectiveness of climate change-related measures.

The Group places high importance on building the cognitive capacity of all employees regarding climate-related risks
and opportunities. This year, we have conducted ESG training for the Board, including dedicated climate sessions, and
invited external experts to share cutting-edge insights. This ensures that the Board possesses the necessary knowledge
and skills to effectively supervise and manage the Company’s strategies for addressing climate risks and opportunities,
continuously enhancing our management level in this area.

deepen the understanding of climate risks across all business units. Based on this understanding, we formulate targeted

During the Reporting Period, the Group comprehensively reviewed the potential financial impacts associated with key
climate-related physical risks, transition risks and opportunities. In terms of physical risks, extreme weather events
could potentially impact boutique footfall and sales, as well as the logistics and transportation of raw materials,
potentially leading to a decline in sales revenue and additional costs related to supply chain disruptions. In terms of
transition risks, the tightening of low-carbon policies and the promotion of green consumption concepts may drive
increased investment in areas such as energy-saving renovations, consequently leading to a temporary increase in
operation-related costs. In terms of climate-related opportunities, the systematic implementation of carbon reduction
strategies can lead to a reduction in operation-related costs and enhance the brand’s competitiveness in the
sustainable consumption market.




                                                                                ANNUAL REPORT 2025   LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD           73
Environmental, Social and Governance Report




Overall, during this year, the impacts mentioned above did not have a significant adverse effect on the Group’s overall
profitability or financial stability. The Company’s current overall financial performance was not materially affected.
Based on current assessments, it is anticipated that these factors will not pose a significant risk of material adjustment
to the carrying amounts of assets and liabilities within the next Reporting Period. Furthermore, considering the stable
and standardized nature of the Company’s business operation model, the effective response measures already
formulated for various risks and opportunities, and the ongoing dynamic monitoring of the evolution of these risks and
opportunities, no significant financial impact is expected in the future.

Risk Management
Addressing climate-related risks has become an integral part of the Group’s comprehensive risk management system.
During this year, we have established and continuously improved an annual identification and assessment mechanism
for climate risks and opportunities:

Risk Identification: Referring to the TCFD (Task Force on Climate-Related Financial Disclosures) analytical framework
and industry practices, combined with research on industry trends, we compile a list of climate risks. Based on our
core business model, through cross-departmental discussions and consultations with external experts, we accurately
identify key climate risks and opportunities, forming a dynamically updated list of climate risks and opportunities.

Risk Assessment: We organize thematic seminars centered on foundational information and the climate risk list,
gathering input from key functional departments and business management. This allows us to systematically assess
the potential impacts of climate factors on our business model, value chain, and financial performance. Based on the
assessment results, high-priority risks are incorporated into the Company’s overall risk ranking and control system,
serving as a key basis for resource allocation and business decisions.

Risk Response: We formulate differentiated response plans for different risks, clearly defining responsible
departments, implementation paths, and timelines. For significant risks, we review the adequacy of resource allocation,
the effectiveness of response measures, and the reasonableness of transition plans, integrating them into the annual
key work plan for coordinated promotion and implementation.

Risk Management Monitoring and Improvement: The Group’s management regularly reviews the implementation
progress and actual effectiveness of climate risk response measures, promptly addressing implementation bottlenecks
and dynamically adjusting strategies to adapt to changes in the internal and external environment. The Board and
its special committees receive a dedicated report on climate risk management at least once a year, tracking the
implementation progress of response measures and the achievement of interim goals, ensuring the standardized and
effective operation of the climate risk management mechanism.

Metrics and Targets
The Group has established a mechanism for the regular collection and calculation of greenhouse gas emission data.
During this year, building upon the coverage of Scope 1 and Scope 2 greenhouse gas emissions, we systematically
advanced the calculation of Scope 3 greenhouse gas emissions. We have collected and calculated greenhouse gas
emission data related to business travel (Scope 3, Category 6). Concurrently, we are continuously advancing the
calculation of greenhouse gas emission data for the remaining categories to compile a complete and accurate Scope 3
greenhouse gas emission inventory, thereby enhancing the transparency of carbon emissions across our entire value
chain.

Environmental Targets
Based on business characteristics and medium-to-long-term development plans, the Group has established
environmental targets covering key areas such as resource efficiency and waste reduction, and is committed to
continuously managing and effectively reducing the negative environmental impacts across all its operational processes.
In addition, the Group has integrated its target planning into the development of a long-term climate governance
system, and will gradually carry out systematic research in light of national policy directions, industry trends, and its
own operational realities. Looking ahead, once the data foundation, management capacity, and internal and external
conditions are stable and mature, the Group will formulate scientifically sound and feasible short-, medium-, and long-
term carbon reduction targets, further improve its climate change management system, and steadily advance its low-
carbon transformation.

•      Resource Efficiency: Conduct energy conservation campaigns, strengthen patrols in production and office areas
       to eliminate waste of water and electricity, and enhance energy efficiency.

•      Waste Reduction: Ensure that 100% of waste is disposed of by qualified third parties.

EMPLOYMENT AND LABOR PRACTICES
Diversity and Equal Opportunity
We are committed to fostering a fair, harmonious, mutually respectful and diverse work environment. We have
established internal policies such as the recruitment management policy, the internal referral talent reward system, and
the employee relations management policy to clarify management requirements for recruitment, onboarding, promotion
and termination, while ensuring a fair and transparent environment to foster talent development. Quantitative data
relating to employment and labor forces is presented in the “ESG Key Performance”.

Our recruitment, compensation and promotion opportunities are not based on an employee’s gender, age, educational
background, marital status, pregnancy, family, disability, race, ethnicity or national origin, nationality, religious beliefs, or
any other non-work-related factors. The Group strictly prohibits any form of discrimination, harassment or defamation in
the workplace for any reason. During the Reporting Period, the Group did not identify any instances of non-compliance
with anti-discrimination regulations.

It is precisely because we possess an excellent corporate culture, a harmonious, equitable and mutually respectful
work environment, as well as a genuine concern for our employees that our employee turnover rate has remained at a
relatively low level during the Reporting Period.

Compensation, Benefits and Protection of Rights and Interests
In accordance with applicable laws and regulations in the locations where we operate such as the Labor Law of the
People’s Republic of China, the Labor Contract Law of the PRC, the Group has established its compensation and benefit
management policy (In addition to paying social insurance and housing provident fund, we provide comprehensive
compensation and benefits, including wages, bonuses, employee care leave and other allowances.) These policies aim
to create a fair, just, reasonable and market-competitive compensation benefits management framework. Additionally,
we implement equity incentive plans to motivate, attract and retain outstanding talents.

On February 26, 2025, the Company adopted the “2025 Share Award Scheme” RSU Scheme upon the approval by the
extraordinary general meeting, proposing to grant up to 9.4775 million H shares (accounting for 10% of the issued H
shares) to the core team. The incentive recipients include Directors (excluding independent non-executive Directors),
Supervisors, and core employees, with a focus on high-performing employees (requiring an “Excellent” performance
rating for the previous year) and key positions (such as senior management and technical backbone), achieving both
breadth and depth of coverage. On February 26, 2025, the Company granted a total of 597,000 Awarded Shares to 27
employees of the Company in accordance with the terms of the RSU Scheme. On April 24, 2025, the Company further
granted a total of 120,000 Awarded Shares to 21 employees (including Ms. HU Binbin (胡彬彬) (the spouse of Mr. SUI Wu
(隋武), a Supervisor of the Company)) of the Company in accordance with the terms of the RSU Scheme. For the above
terms and details, please refer to the Company’s circular dated February 11, 2025 and the Company’s announcements
dated February 26, 2025 and April 24, 2025.

We request department heads to allocate human resources judiciously to maximize work efficiency, avoid unnecessary
overtime, and safeguard the legitimate rights and interests of employees. The Group formulates internal policies based
on local employment laws, which serve as the basis for determining reasonable working hours and leave entitlements
for employees. In accordance with relevant laws, regulations and internal policies, such as the Attendance and Leave
Management Policy, the Group, including its headquarter, stores and factory, will select working hour systems and
leave standards for relevant positions based on actual circumstances.

In compliance with national laws and regulations as well as our corporate culture, we have established a flexible and
humane leave system to safeguard employee rights and benefits, which includes monthly leave, annual leave, statutory
holidays, marriage leave, maternity leave, prenatal check-up leave, nursing leave, parental leave, sick leave, personal
affair leave, bereavement leave, study leave, and various other types of leave benefits. We actively foster a harmonious
and inclusive work environment, while steadfastly upholding our commitment to the well-being of our employees.

We are committed to creating a healthy, inclusive, and warm workplace ecosystem through comprehensive care
initiatives, so that every employee can feel warmth and a sense of belonging while realizing their self-worth. At the
same time, we have built a multi-channel, full-loop employee communication and feedback mechanism, including
various communication channels such as a grievance procedure, complaint email, and suggestion box, and we regularly
conduct employee satisfaction/engagement surveys. We actively respond to and promptly improve upon employee
feedback, ensuring that employees’ voices are heard and valued.

Occupational Health and Safety
We attach great importance to occupational and workplace safety and have complied with the relevant applicable
laws and regulations in the locations where we operate concerning the provision of a safe working environment and
the protection of employees from occupational hazards, which may have a significant impact on the Group, including
but not limited to the Law of the PRC on the Prevention and Treatment of Occupational Diseases and the Occupational
Safety and Health Ordinance in Hong Kong. To ensure production safety, we have established a comprehensive and
effective safety production management policy, and have formulated various standard operating procedures such as
the Work Safety Management Manual, the Emergency Response Plan for Work Safety Accidents, and the Dual System
of Management and Control of Risk Classification and Hidden Risk Investigation and Treatment to regulate safety
production management. Quantitative data relating to occupational health and safety is presented in the “ESG Key
Performance”.

We also place great emphasis on safety training to enhance employees’ safety awareness. We conduct regular safety
production training and daily safety inspections, performing comprehensive safety check once every month to identify
and rectify any potential safety hazards.

We have also rigorously implemented the work safety responsibility system. We regularly conduct safety education,
training, inspections and evaluations to our employees, especially providing pre-job training for production employees,
helping them understand our product manufacturing processes, and strictly adhere to operational procedures,
and provide them with protective equipment. We continuously enhance the safety production responsibilities and
awareness of our employees, thereby effectively preventing the occurrence of safety incidents, and conduct regular
safety inspections to identify potential risks. With a view to standardizing the management, reporting and evaluation of
safety incidents, we have established a set of criteria to classify safety incidents according to their severity. Disciplinary
actions against the directly responsible personnel, supervisory personnel and leadership personnel will be determined
based on the severity of the incident.

In fire safety management, we strictly comply with the Fire Prevention Law of the PRC. Our leased office spaces
and retail stores are equipped with essential firefighting facilities, and we adhere to the fire safety regulations of
the respective office buildings and shopping malls where they are located. At our self-owned factories, we have
implemented a comprehensive fire prevention and firefighting system designed to trigger alarms and activate fire
suppression procedures immediately upon fire detection. An emergency notification system is also in place to ensure
swift communication of critical information during emergencies. We engage professional third-party to perform daily
maintenance of our fire protection systems. This includes regular inspections and performance tests of firefighting
equipment.

In addition, we have established various guidelines and policies to ensure workplace safety in our stores, such as the
Boutique Safety Management Policy, the Contingency Plan for Fire Control and the Contingency Plan for Boutique
Theft. While preventing potential safety incidents in stores from causing property and asset losses, we are committed
to providing a safe working and shopping environment for both boutique employees and customers. We also conduct
emergency drills for our employees from time to time.

Meanwhile, we have established an annual employee health examination program to consistently monitor the physical
well-being of our employees and provide guidance for maintaining a healthy working environment.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report




Development and Training
The development and progress of our employees are crucial to our success. To facilitate employee growth, we have
established a comprehensive training system, providing employees with a range of training opportunities, including
new employee orientation training, specialized training, internal mentor training, external institution training, online
courses, etc. The training content covers the Company’s code of conduct, anti-corruption and anti-money laundering,
information security, legal compliance, health and safety, and professional training skills for various functional
departments. In our daily production activities, we implement an apprenticeship system to provide technical and
knowledge training for new employees. Quantitative data related to training and development is presented in the “ESG
Key Performance”.

At the same time, we provide employees with fair, just and smooth promotion pathways, covering a dual-path
promotion model for professional skills and management capabilities. We have clarified corresponding promotion
standards based on the characteristics of each position and formulated the Performance Appraisal Management
System and the Promotion and Demotion System to regularly assess and evaluate employees’ work performance,
abilities, attitudes, and other work aspects over a certain period. The performance appraisal of our front-line employees
is not strongly correlated with sales performance. We place greater emphasis on the service quality of front-line
employees, striving to provide customers with high-quality and consistent service.

We also strictly prohibit any form of unfair or unjust dismissal and have established strict policies to govern dismissal
procedures.

Labor Standards
The Group has strictly adhered to the labor laws and regulations promulgated by the countries and regions where it
operates, including the Provisions on the Prohibition of Using Child Labour and the Law of the PRC on the Protection of
Minors, so as to prohibit the employment of any child or forced labor.

In terms of prohibiting the employment of child labor, we strictly verify the relevant identity information of applicants
during both the recruitment and onboarding processes to confirm they meet the legal working age; in terms of
preventing forced labor, we prioritize the autonomy of our employees and their vacation arrangements, while respecting
their reasonable working hours and holidays. We strictly prohibit restricting employees’ personal freedom or coercing
labor through methods such as collecting deposits, withholding documents, or conducting threats. When employees
leave, we respect their freedom to choose new career opportunities. During the Reporting Period, the Group did not
identify any significant instances of non-compliance or violations of laws and regulations pertaining to the prohibition of
child labor and forced labor.

SUPPLY CHAIN MANAGEMENT
Responsible procurement and sound supply chain management are essential to ensure reliable product quality and
the sustainability of our supply chain. To promote sustainable and responsible practices, we have established various
management policies for suppliers, including the Quality Standards for Gold Raw Materials and Gold Products, the
Management System for Gold Procurement and Processing, the Quality Standards and Quality Inspection Procedures
for Raw Materials, and the Management System for Production and Processing Suppliers. Quantitative data related to
supply chain management is presented in the “ESG Key Performance”.

Qualification Review
Under these policies, in addition to considering factors such as infrastructure, production capacity and market
reputation, we also evaluate potential suppliers’ performance in terms of legal, ethical, environmental and social
compliance. All suppliers must undergo our established evaluation process prior to formal procurement. The supplier
review rate for 2025 was 100%.

We require suppliers to comply with the laws of the countries and regions where they are located and prohibit the
employment of any child labor, involuntary labor, etc., so as to ensure the protection of employee rights throughout the
supply chain. Given equal qualifications, we will give priority to suppliers who provide environmentally friendly products
and services. During supplier access, we evaluate suppliers through due diligence and on-site inspections to ensure
their compliance with environmental, social and governance regulations.

The Group maintains partnerships with multiple suppliers to mitigate the risk of over-dependence. The Group requires
suppliers, through signed agreements, to respect and comply with our compliance policies, including anti-bribery
policies, to support fair competition.

We firmly oppose the procurement of raw materials such as gold and diamonds originating from conflict zones,
involving illegal mining, infringing on human rights, or violating environmental regulations, to strictly guard against the
risk of unethical jewelry raw materials related to crime, armed conflict, or human rights violations. In the case of gold
raw material suppliers and diamond raw material suppliers, we require that such suppliers possess membership in the
Shanghai Gold Exchange (if they are domestic gold raw material suppliers) or the Shanghai Diamond Exchange (if they
are domestic diamond raw material suppliers), as well as a robust internal financial accounting system. These national-
level institutions typically impose stringent requirements on the gold and diamonds provided by their qualified suppliers.
We believe by virtue of their strong supervision and surveillance, we are able to ensure the legality of sources of gold
and diamond that we procure from our suppliers.

Supplier Evaluation
As for the suppliers with whom we have established cooperative relationships, we conduct evaluations and
categorizations for our suppliers based on the duration of collaboration, qualifications, and the quality of raw materials
and products on a monthly and annual basis. We will also require lower-tier suppliers to implement corrective actions
until they pass our re-evaluation. Additionally, we have formulated specific standards for the quality, dimension, weight,
and appearance of raw materials and products. Our quality inspectors are responsible for examining raw materials and
products in accordance with these established standards. Upon discovery of any non-compliant products or materials,
we will make every effort to collaborate with suppliers to seek a resolution. But if no improvement is made thereafter,
the relevant supplier will be warned, temporarily suspended, or even permanently disqualified from receiving orders.




                                                                           ANNUAL REPORT 2025   LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD     79
Environmental, Social and Governance Report




ANTI-CORRUPTION AND ANTI-MONEY LAUNDERING
We attach great importance to building a culture of anti-corruption and integrity, strictly adhere to anti-corruption and
anti-bribery laws and regulations, while consistently upholding the principles of honest practice, integrity in operations, as
well as fairness and justice. We have established policies such as the Company Charter, the Employee Work Discipline,
Anti-Fraud Management Policy and Anti-Money Laundering Policy to ensure the standardization of employee conduct.
Every new employee must participate in an orientation training before taking the post, study relevant regulations, and
confirm their understanding. During the Reporting Period, we conducted training on anti-corruption, codes of conduct,
etc., for the Company’s Directors and employees.

Additionally, we require our suppliers to respect and adhere to our compliance policies, including our anti-bribery
policy. We have also established stringent control procedures in this regard to prevent corruption and dishonesty in
execution, including dual verification, multi-level reviews, and periodic inspections.

We place a high value on integrity and anti-fraud measures, and foster a culture of compliance. We consistently
maintain a high-pressure stance against disciplinary violations, and actively formulate the Anti-Fraud Management
Practices. We have established rigorous anti-fraud measures in the prevention, reporting and investigation of
misconduct. We periodically conduct anti-fraud corporate culture activities to ensure that all employees are informed
of our policies on fraud prevention and their own responsibilities in combating fraud. We have established a fraud risk
assessment system and formulated corresponding internal control measures. Our internal control and audit department
takes the lead in identifying fraud risks on an annual basis.

We have established a whistle-blower hotline and an email address for corruption and fraudulent activities. We actively
encourage employees to report any suspected fraudulent or illegal activities, and ensure that all reported cases are
thoroughly and promptly investigated. Upon detection of any suspected fraudulent activities, our internal control and
audit departments will collaborate with our legal, human resources and office personnel to conduct a preliminary
assessment, form an investigation team, and compile an investigative report. We have established and implemented
a whistle-blower protection mechanism, conducting investigations into complaints and reports in accordance with the
principles of fairness, impartiality, and confidentiality. Whistle-blowers and the information they provide will be kept
strictly confidential.

During the Reporting Period, we did not identify any significant violations of laws, regulations or internal anti-corruption
and anti-fraudulent requirements. In the future, we will continue to strictly adhere to current requirements, while
maintaining a clean and fair environment for business operations and development.

We actively comply with relevant laws and regulations, including the Anti-Money Laundering Law of the People’s
Republic of China, the Administrative Measures for Anti-Money Laundering and Counter-Terrorism Financing for
Payment Institutions, as well as related overseas institutional provisions. And we have established the Anti-Money
Laundering Management Policy to cover all operational aspects that may be used for money laundering in our business.
During the Reporting Period, we have not been subject to any lawsuits, disputes, legal procedures, claims, investigations
or other actions by any regulatory authorities regarding anti-money laundering matters.

COMMUNITY PARTICIPATION
We firmly believe that a corporate’s long-term value is not only reflected in economic benefits but also in its sustained
contribution and positive impact on society. The Group actively undertakes its social responsibilities, never forgetting to
give back to the society while advancing corporate development.

We encourage our employees to actively participate in public welfare activities, such as children education, social
volunteer services, care for vulnerable groups in the community, community environmental protection services, and
promotion of Chinese classical culture, and make charitable donations across multiple fields. In November 2025,
Laopu Gold donated HK$10 million to urgently support the fire rescue in Wang Fuk Court in Tai Po District, Hong Kong,
helping affected residents overcome difficulties. As of the end of the Reporting Period, our total public welfare donation
amounted to RMB10,306 thousand.

In the future, we will actively explore more diverse social welfare activities and forms, giving back to society through
practical actions.

                 ASSURANCE STATEMENT
                 REPORT ON SUSTAINABILITY ACTIVITIES IN THE LAOPU GOLD CO., LTD.’S
                 ESG REPORT FOR 2025
                 NATURE OF THE ASSURANCE/VERIFICATION
                 SGS-CSTC Standards Technical Services Co., Ltd. (hereinafter referred to as SGS-CSTC) was commissioned
                 by Laopu Gold Co., Ltd. (hereinafter referred to as Laopu Gold) to conduct an independent assurance on the
                 partial sustainability performance information included in the Laopu Gold’s ESG Report for 2025 (Chinese version)
                 for the period of January 1, 2025 to December 31, 2025.

                 INTENDED USERS OF THIS ASSURANCE STATEMENT
                 This Assurance Statement is provided with the intention of informing all Laopu Gold’s Stakeholders.

                 RESPONSIBILITIES
                 The sustainability information in the ESG Report for 2025 and its presentation are the responsibility of Laopu
                 Gold’s ESG governing body and the management. SGS-CSTC has not been involved in the preparation of any
                 of the material included in the Laopu Gold’s ESG Report for 2025.

                 Our responsibility is to express an opinion on the sustainability performance information within the scope of
                 assurance based upon sufficient and appropriate objective evidence.

                 SGS-CSTC hereby states that it shall not be held responsible or liable for any direct, indirect, incidental, or
                 consequential damages or losses arising from or in connection with the use of information provided in this report.

                 ASSURANCE STANDARDS, TYPE AND LEVEL OF ASSURANCE
                 The SGS Group ESG & Sustainability Report Assurance (SRA) protocols used to conduct assurance are based
                 upon internationally recognised assurance standards including the ISAE 3000.

                 The assurance of this report has been conducted according to the following Assurance Standards:
                  Assurance Standard                                                       Level of Assurance
                  ISAE 3000                                                                Limited

                 ASSURANCE METHODOLOGY
                 The assurance comprised a combination of pre-assurance research, interviews with relevant employees on-site
                 at 6/F, No. 3 West Building, The Towers at Oriental Plaza, No. 1 Dong Chang’an Avenue, Dongcheng District,
                 Beijing City, P.R. China, including documentation and record review and validation where relevant.

                 The procedures performed in a limited assurance engagement vary in nature and timing from, and are less in
                 extent than for, a reasonable assurance engagement. Consequently, the level of assurance obtained in a limited
                 assurance engagement is lower than the assurance that would have been obtained had a reasonable assurance
                 engagement been performed.

                 LIMITATIONS AND MITIGATION
                 Data drawn directly from independently audited financial accounts and intensity data calculated based on financial
                 data has not been checked back to source as part of this assurance process.

                 The greenhouse gas emissions related data in the Laopu Gold’s ESG Report for 2025 was calculated by Laopu
                 Gold. In the context of the present assurance engagement, our procedures were limited to sample-based
                 validation.

                 STATEMENT OF INDEPENDENCE AND COMPETENCE
                 The SGS Group of companies is the world leader in inspection, testing and certification, operating in multiple
                 countries and providing services. As an affiliate of SGS Group, SGS-CSTC affirm our independence from Laopu
                 Gold, being free from bias and conflicts of interest with the organisation, its subsidiaries and stakeholders.

                 The assurance team was assembled based on their knowledge, experience and qualifications for this assignment.

                 FINDINGS AND CONCLUSIONS

                 ASSURANCE/VERIFICATION OPINION
                 On the basis of the methodology described and the assurance engagement performed, no inaccuracies or
                 reliability issues were identified within the scope of the sustainability performance information covered by the
                 Laopu Gold’s ESG Report for 2025.
